On this shortened backbone, a decision head scores each candidate by pretrained query--key matching and refines the scores with a lightweight multilayer perceptron (MLP) residual, coupling the pretrained matching structure with task-specific nonlinear discrimination. Built on Qwen3.5-4B, \textsc{MedJev}-4B improves both accuracy and speed over the original model on each of four medical image classification and visual question answering benchmarks, with an absolute accuracy gain of \textbf{7.93\%} and a \textbf{6.04$\times$} end-to-end speedup. Further comparison with 20 cutting-edge models shows that \textsc{MedJev}-4B achieves state-of-the-art performance and inference speed among 4B--9B general and medical VLMs, indicating that direct multimodal decision making is an effective route to fast and effective medical image understanding.
  </details>

- **[FAVR: Flow-Aware Visual Residual Alignment for Vision-Language-Action Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20971)**  `arXiv:2611.20971`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qixuan Wu, Xiaole Yang, Yuguang Yang, Canyu Chen, Linlin Yang, Haixia Pan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pretrained video representations provide rich temporal information for robotic manipulation, yet effectively integrating them into existing vision-language-action (VLA) policies remains challenging. We present Flow-Aware Visual Residual Alignment (FAVR), a lightweight framework for flow-aware visual conditioning of frozen VLA policies. FAVR maintains a compact temporal visual state through linear Test-Time Training (TTT) and retrieves action-conditioned visual features throughout flow-based action generation. By exploiting the affine structure of the frozen action head, we explicitly characterize how injected visual features influence predicted action velocities and formulate flow-aware channel selection as a box-constrained optimization problem. We develop a FISTA-based solver for joint gate optimization and derive an efficient closed-form approximation through diagonalization and rescaling. With only 1.74-2.19M trainable parameters and 5,000 adaptation steps, FAVR improves pretrained VLA policies across benchmarks. On RoboTwin 2.0, FAVR improves LingBot-VLA from 87.12% to 90.04% under clean conditions and from 86.88% to 89.38% under randomized conditions. A controlled ablation on five RoboTwin tasks further shows that flow-aware gating increases average success from 72.0% with a gate shared across flow steps to 82.0%, demonstrating the benefit of adapting visual conditioning throughout action generation.
  </details>

- **[EviBridge: Encoding Predictive Visual Evidence for Proactive Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20916)**  `arXiv:2611.20916`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhichao Huang, Changheng Cai, Song Ren, Zhichen Zhao, Hongsen Liu, Chaoyang Zheng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Tool-handover interaction calls for preparation before a response is warranted. The model waits until interaction cues appear, then prepares while assessing whether the accumulating evidence supports a response or calls for cancellation. We introduce EviBridge, a framework that integrates predictive visual representations into a vision-language interaction model for joint state and tool prediction. A trainable evidence interface maps features from a frozen V-JEPA 2 encoder into the language decoder alongside native visual inputs, preserving their temporal context and using only observations available at each decision time. The framework has two instantiations. EviBridge-E distinguishes recent from historical evidence and encodes the timing and spatial location of each observation. EviBridge-I preserves the temporal order of pooled V-JEPA 2 features and interaction turns and generates dedicated interaction tokens. We evaluate on FuGIBench, a streaming-video benchmark for preparation, response, and cancellation in tool-handover assistance. The best-performing variants of EviBridge-E and EviBridge-I achieve Overall scores of 0.6448 and 0.5892, compared with 0.2178 and 0.5327 for their respective task-adapted native baselines. These gains span target handovers, long waits, and cancelled requests, demonstrating the value of predictive visual evidence for interaction decisions.
  </details>

- **[Can Image Generators Hold a Scene Together When Space Is Broken Apart?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20906)**  `arXiv:2611.20906`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jhen-Ke Lin, Chung-Chun Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image generators are good at drawing scenes in which things that belong together appear next to each other. In almost every image they generate, and in almost every benchmark used to evaluate these images, "belonging to the same whole" and "adjacent to each other" go together. Therefore, we cannot know whether the model truly grasps the scene as a whole, or simply makes each part connect with its adjacent parts. We propose GJBench to disentangle the two. It requires the model to draw a scene as a scattered jigsaw puzzle: an image is cut into interlocking pieces, then moved apart and rotated. Thus, the spatial plausibility of the scene exists only in how these pieces collectively point to a whole. The model may have a reference image, a target layout, both, or neither. We separately evaluate three requirements: whether the scene is broken down into a specified number of separate pieces, whether the pieces interlock, and whether the pieces can reconstruct the reference scene. While every open and closed model we evaluated could draw these scenes well as a whole, none could keep them together as scattered puzzle pieces, and the three requirements categorized failures into different patterns. The models draw separate pieces but miscount them in different ways, and unless their outlines are provided, the pieces rarely interlock, and their content does not reconstruct the reference image. We also found that the target layout helps the puzzle pieces fit together, while the reference image improves neither their count nor their fit. These findings show that keeping a scene continuous across space that the canvas no longer shows remains an open problem for image generators.
  </details>

- **[SciWorldBench: Do Video World Models Really Understand Scientific Experiments?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20899)**  `arXiv:2611.20899`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xue Yang, Haonan Cai, Xinyu Hang, Zhimeng Huang, Xuanyi Liu, Chuanmin Jia, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While the ability of video generation models to accurately depict scientific phenomena has received growing attention, determining whether these models genuinely understand the underlying principles remains a critical challenge. Existing benchmarks typically evaluate video samples in isolation, making it difficult to expose models that simply fit superficial visual priors. In scientific experiments, a small change in one condition produces a markedly different outcome even when the rest of the setup remains fixed. Capitalizing on this sensitivity, we introduce SciWorldBench, a benchmark comprising paired scientific experiments. Each pair varies a single condition in an otherwise fixed setup, yielding two mutually exclusive expected outcomes. Each generation prompt specifies the condition but omits the expected outcome. We evaluate ten text-and-image-to-video (TI2V) models using an independent vision-language model (VLM) that assesses each video without access to the condition text or the label identifying the correct outcome. A pair is scored as correct only when both videos depict their respective scientifically correct outcomes. Across all ten models, we find that the two videos in an experimental pair frequently depict the same outcome even though the conditions require different results. This recurring failure suggests that current video world models struggle to apply the underlying scientific principles reliably during generation. We will publicly release the benchmark dataset and open-source the evaluation code.
  </details>

- **[ProSE: Probing Reconstruction Error for Semantic-guided Expert Skipping in MoE-MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20885)**  `arXiv:2611.20885`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiameng Li, Minye Wu, Matthew B. Blaschko_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Mixture-of-Experts Multimodal Large Language Models (MoE-MLLMs) offer scalable inference through sparse expert activation, yet substantial computational redundancy remains among the activated experts. Existing expert skipping methods typically calibrate thresholds based on target skipping ratios or final-output divergence, overlooking the accumulation of skipping-induced error across layers and the varying semantic importance of individual tokens. To address that, we propose ProSE, a training-free, semantic-guided expert skipping framework that probes layer-wise reconstruction error to promote representation consistency compared with default activation. Specifically, we construct query-aware semantic masks to identify salient visual tokens and progressively calibrate modality-specific skipping thresholds for each MoE layer. By minimizing masked reconstruction error under a given budget, ProSE maintains semantic consistency after skipping and allows adaptive expert allocation under limited activation budgets. Extensive experiments on 9 benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements on average over existing expert skipping methods, particularly under aggressive skipping settings. ProSE maintains over 96\% performance on KimiVL-A3B-Inst with 83\% expert skipping and accelerates prefilling by $1.7\times$.
  </details>

- **[When Is the Expensive Method Worth It? A Content-Driven Analysis of Classical Bayer Demosaicing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20854)**  `arXiv:2611.20854`  `cs.CV`  
  _Taranum Wasu, Harsh Kashyap, HS Pannu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Demosaicing reconstructs a full-colour image from a single-sensor colour-filter-array (CFA) mosaic, where only one colour is measured per pixel. Classical methods make different assumptions about images, from simple averaging to directional edge-aware interpolation, and the directional methods cost markedly more compute. We ask a practical question that a per-image quality ranking does not answer: for a given image, is the expensive directional method worth its cost? Using a controlled simulate-reconstruct pipeline on 80 benchmark images from four public datasets (Kodak, McMaster, BSD100, Urban100), six classical methods, and image-content descriptors fixed before any results were seen, we show that the quality gain of the edge-aware method (Menon 2007) over the best cheap linear method is not constant. It ranges from $-6.6$ to $+6.8$ dB, and on 31% of images it is zero or negative, meaning a cheap method matches or beats the expensive one. The gain is also predictable from content: it correlates with mean image saturation at $r=-0.86$ ($p<10^{-6}$). We then build an interpretable depth-2 decision tree that recommends the best cheap method from image descriptors, beating an always-pick-the-most-common baseline by 20.0 percentage points under leave-one-image-out validation. The analysis uses no deep learning, and every step can be inspected.
  </details>

- **[STARS: Stage-Adaptive Responsibility Separation for Shadow Removal in Remote Sensing Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20698)**  `arXiv:2611.20698`  `cs.CV`  
  _Gaoliang Song, Chengjie Li, Pengju Liu, Chenchen Chang, Jinhao You, Hongzhi Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Shadows are a ubiquitous radiative attenuation phenomenon in remote sensing images and can severely affect high-resolution remote sensing interpretation tasks. In shadow removal, although introducing a mask prior provides explicit positional information for the restoration network, it also causes non-shadow regions to be modulated by the shadow prior, producing a systematic brightness shift; notably, this phenomenon is independent of whether the mask is accurate. In remote sensing images, non-shadow regions usually occupy a larger proportion, so a small shift over these regions incurs a much higher cost on whole-image metrics than an equal gain in shadow regions. Adopting a task-decoupling perspective, we therefore decompose shadow removal into two tasks, shadow-region restoration and non-shadow-region identity, within a cascaded architecture, and handle them sequentially. Based on this insight, we propose STARS, a cascaded shadow removal framework that separates regional responsibilities across stages. The method retains the advantages of the shadow mask while resolving the degradation of non-shadow regions, without requiring a complex parallel dual-branch structure. Meanwhile, to introduce large-model prior knowledge into remote sensing tasks, we further design a Feature Selection and Fusion Adapter (FSFA). We conduct extensive experiments on three benchmark datasets, UAV-SC+, ISTD+ and WSRD+ 2026, demonstrating the superiority of STARS. The framework shows that decoupling regional responsibilities can resolve the intrinsic contradiction of mask priors, providing a new direction for image restoration with region heterogeneity.
  </details>

- **[Counterfactual Feature Steering for Adjacency-Preserving Identity Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20637)**  `arXiv:2611.20637`  `cs.CV`  
  _FNU Shivam, Layth Sliman, Ramana Reddy, Donald Adjeroh, Binod Bhattarai, Prashnna Gyawali_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Identity unlearning in text-to-image diffusion models is increasingly important for privacy, safety, and ethical deployment, as it aims to remove the model's ability to generate specific individuals. In this work, we study identity unlearning with \emph{adjacency preservation}, where the goal is to remove a target individual's identity while preserving related non-target identities. We propose Counterfactual Feature Steering (CFS), a method requiring one-time SAE training that performs bounded inference-time concept editing in the latent space of a sparse autoencoder attached to the text encoder. CFS derives a target direction by contrasting forget and retain distributions, then applies a per-token projection proportional to each token's current alignment with this direction, yielding counterfactual representations that suppress the target while limiting collateral damage. To evaluate adjacency preservation, we introduce the Adjacency-Weighted Unlearning Score (AWUS), which measures the tradeoff between target erasure and collateral damage, and extend it to face-specific identity similarity using ArcFace. Across 4,000 prompts derived from Celeb-100, spanning 100 celebrity identities, CFS achieves the strongest evaluated tradeoff between identity erasure of target identities and preservation of adjacent and generic concepts, while remaining stable across intervention strengths where other steering-based methods degrade. Independent ArcFace evaluation and a 10,000-caption COCO study further examine identity and general preservation. Quantitative nudity-suppression benchmarks and qualitative artistic-style examples provide evidence of transfer beyond identities on SD~1.4.
  </details>

- **[PixelBench: Evaluating Language Models for Pixel-Art Creation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20628)**  `arXiv:2611.20628`  `cs.CV`  
  _Mutian Hong, Jianxuan Yao, Mingkun Yue, Changkai Li, Hower Yang, Haibin Ling_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pixel art is widely used in games and digital content creation, requiring readable forms, coherent spatial relationships, and harmonious colors under limited resolution and palette constraints. Language models show growing promise in visual creation, yet their pixel art creation capabilities lack systematic evaluation. We introduce PixelBench, formalizing text-to-image generation and instruction-guided editing for pixel art and providing a benchmark for these tasks. We further present a creation environment in which language model agents invoke drawing tools through code to generate and edit pixel art. We assess the quality of generated artwork through human evaluation and employ a multimodal large language model as a judge, validating the agreement between its automated assessments and human preferences. We compare language models and configurations across diverse subjects and canvas sizes. In our experiments, GPT-6 Astra achieves both the highest average judge score and the best average human rank, highlighting the potential of general-purpose language models for pixel art creation. We release the tasks, all artworks, the human rankings, and the judge protocol.
  </details>

- **[Example-Based Spatial Guidance for Training-Free Concept Erasure in Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20623)**  `arXiv:2611.20623`  `cs.CV`  
  _Younghwan Kil, Giung Nam, Joonhyeong Park, Jinwoo Shin, Juho Lee_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models often synthesize policy-violating content, necessitating robust safeguards and rigorous evaluation frameworks. However, current approaches remain largely monolithic in both execution and evaluation, limited to coarse-grained categorization within broad unsafe concepts. Such approaches fail to account for the specific constituent factors of a violation, resulting in weak learning signals and allowing model collapse to bypass safety checks. To overcome these limitations, we propose Example-Based Spatial Guidance (EBSG), a training-free method that utilizes user-editable exemplar packs to provide granular spatial guidance for precise concept steering. Unlike existing methods that coarsely steer the entire image away from a broad concept, EBSG decomposes these categories into specific sub-concepts via exemplar text-image pairs, providing explicit, localized steering signals for more robust safety control. Furthermore, we introduce a vision language model (VLM)-based evaluation protocol that provides a fine-grained assessment, avoiding the pitfall of conventional binary evaluators that permit model collapse to bypass safety checks. Empirically, EBSG achieves state-of-the-art erasure performance across diverse safety datasets and concept categories, spanning four nudity benchmarks, seven I2P harmful-concept slices, and four MJA metaphor categories, while supporting multi-concept removal and transferring to a modern backbone, SD3.
  </details>

- **[Learning a Task-Adaptive Low-Dimensional Semantic Space for Improved Visual Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20553)**  `arXiv:2611.20553`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nikalal Kaldera, Wanqing Li, Chris Bunn, Philip O. Ogunbona_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Semantic visual classification commonly aligns visual embeddings with semantic spaces induced by pretrained text encoders. However, such spaces are typically high-dimensional, weakly aligned with visual feature geometry, and insufficiently discriminative for downstream classification tasks. We propose TaSS, a framework for learning a task-adaptive low-dimensional semantic space that preserves semantic structure while explicitly optimizing class separability. TaSS is constructed through a parameterized geometry transformation of pretrained text embeddings that enlarges inter-class margins via distance reshaping and improves intra-class compactness through prototype-preserving semantic constraints. The resulting semantic space maintains meaningful semantic relationships while producing a discriminative geometry tailored for classification. Frozen visual encoder features are subsequently aligned to TaSS using a lightweight MLP, enabling improved discriminative learning without fine-tuning pretrained backbones. We further provide theoretical analysis showing that TaSS preserves semantic consistency while enforcing enhanced inter-class separation and controlled intra-class variance. Extensive experiments on skeleton-based human action recognition, video classification, and image classification benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements over existing semantic alignment approaches, with gains exceeding 3 percentage points on average and up to 8.5 points on challenging datasets.
  </details>

- **[AFRNet: Adaptive Frequency Redistribution Network for Image Deraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20522)**  `arXiv:2611.20522`  `cs.CV`  
  _Dezhi Zhang, Shangrong Yang, Husheng Guo, Zhijie Shen, Wenjian Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image deraining aims to recover rain-free images from rain-degraded observations. Recent frequency-aware deraining methods have demonstrated promising performance by removing rain artifacts in the spectral domain. However, since rain artifacts and background textures often appear in several correlated subbands, prior frequency-domain deraining models still enhance frequency subbands separately. Such band-wise processing limits information exchange across subbands. Without context from other subbands, rain and clean content that appear together in one subband cannot be separated. This leads to either residual rain streaks or over-smoothing of fine details. To address this limitation, we propose AFRNet, an Adaptive Frequency Redistribution Network for image deraining. First, we introduce an Adaptive Frequency Recombination Module (AFRM) that performs cross-band modulation among wavelet subbands, enabling information interaction and adaptive recombination of degraded frequency components. We then develop a Subband Compensation Block (SCB) that compensates for decoder subband degradation by leveraging encoder subband features through inter-subband attention in the wavelet domain, alleviating detail loss during reconstruction. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AFRNet achieves competitive performance and consistent results across diverse deraining benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[What the Averages Hide: Monocular Metric Depth Models Still Fail in Everyday Scenes, Mostly on Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20518)**  `arXiv:2611.20518`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hualie Jiang, Yujie Luo, Ziyang Song, Ximeng Li, Zhiqiang Lou, Rui Xu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Zero-shot monocular metric depth models are evaluated by averaging metric and scale-aligned scores across datasets. Averages cannot show how many images still have severely wrong metric depth, whether depth is under- or overestimated, or whether different models fail on the same images. We propose an evaluation built on per-image results: the failure rate (metric $\delta_1$ < 0.5); scale-aligned metrics for structure, and the bias, dispersion and absolute error of the alignment scale for scale; and a cross-model comparison that separates shared from model-specific failures. We apply it to 11 recent architectures with and without ground-truth intrinsics (18 model evaluations) on the 7 everyday-scene benchmarks (4,406 images) used by MoGe-2. Metric depth estimation is not solved in everyday scenes, and the remaining difficulty is mainly scale: the best of the 18 evaluations still has a failure rate of 7.7% (datasets weighted equally); pooled over all failures, 78.8% have intact structure, and after scale correction 98.3% no longer fail. Images failed by at least 6 architectures make up 7.3% of the data, concentrated on HAMMER and DIODE; even when selected only by architectures of other families, they hold 34% to 40% of the error of the top 6 models, against 14% to 18% for as many random images from the same datasets. Adding objects reduces the scale error of most models; ground-truth intrinsics and, in most families, larger models reduce the failures but do not remove them. We recommend reporting failure rates, structure and scale separately, and results stratified by how many models fail. We will release the code, the per-image results with shared-failure labels, and two diagnostic sets built from TUM RGB-D.
  </details>

- **[How Reliable Is Visual Token Selection? Rethinking Pruning in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20505)**  `arXiv:2611.20505`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chaehee Song, Seokil Ham, KiHyun Seol, Jihyun Lee, Yubin Choi, Changick Kim_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual token pruning improves the efficiency of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by removing redundant visual tokens, yet the reliability of existing selection signals has not been systematically examined. We analyze token selection under semantics-preserving perturbations and across representative pruning criteria, revealing two limitations. First, attention-based importance shifts non-uniformly under perturbations, so magnitude alone cannot distinguish stable evidence from unstable saliency (local instability). Second, attention- and diversity-based methods select substantially different subsets despite comparable performance, showing that useful evidence is not confined to a unique subset (global inconsistency). These findings motivate reliable token-set construction. We instantiate this perspective in CoRePruner, a training-free framework combining instruction-aware diversity with reliability-aware attention. Across image benchmarks, multiple MLLM architectures, and token budgets, CoRePruner improves performance, especially under aggressive pruning, and remains competitive on video benchmarks. Our results establish reliability as a central consideration in visual token selection.
  </details>

- **[LEDGE: Separating Critic and Editor Failures in Text-to-Image Self-Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20446)**  `arXiv:2611.20446`  `cs.CV`  
  _Vyom Agarwal, Mokshda Gangrade, Samet Ayhan, Giacinto Paolo Saggese_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-image (T2I) self-correction combines a vision-language model (VLM) critic that diagnoses errors and proposes instructions with an image editor that executes them. End-to-end evaluation obscures whether failures arise from incorrect diagnoses or an editor unable to execute valid instructions. We introduce LEDGE (Learning Error Diagnosis in Generation and Editing), a framework that separates these bottlenecks. LEDGE compiles oracle edit instructions from GenEval while holding the generated images and the editor fixed, isolating the editor's correction capability from the critic's diagnostic accuracy. On Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large, oracle instructions raise GenEval from 72.0% to 81.2%, versus 74.1% for the baseline VLM critic. Yet 435 of 644 failed images remain unrepaired, exposing a substantial editor gap. We also use oracle labels from disjoint training prompts to supervise the critic. Oracle supervision raises exact diagnosis from 33.3% (the same VLM with our structured output, untrained) to 70.8%. With the same editor, the resulting critic improves GenEval from 42.2% (uncorrected) to 59.4% on Stable Diffusion 1.5, against 50.9% for the baseline critic, and from 72.0% to 76.0% on Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large, despite training only on Stable Diffusion 1.5. Improvements transfer to unseen object classes, a second editor, and independent T2I-CompBench judges and prompts. A small human evaluation confirms the ranking in estimated net corrections but suggests a smaller gain than detector-based evaluation. LEDGE thus distinguishes two limits of self-correction: knowing what to fix and being able to fix it.
  </details>

- **[ReScene: Incremental Scene Chronology from Sparse Revisits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20433)**  `arXiv:2611.20433`  `cs.CV`  
  _Wei Qin Chuah, Zeji Hui, Amirali Khodadadian Gostar, Alireza Bab-Hadiashar_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Scene chronology aims to reconstruct a scene across visits separated by hours or days, during which objects may change in position, presence or shape. Such reconstruction must retain every earlier state, yet each revisit often provides only a few images. Storing a complete model for each visit duplicates the unchanged scene, whereas updating a single shared model alters earlier states. We present ReScene, which stores each visit as an immutable compact update, so that any earlier visit can be recovered exactly by applying the stored updates in order, without retaining past images. To localise each update, ReScene attributes the disagreement between the preceding reconstruction and the new images to individual Gaussians through their compositing weights. This attribution guides the suppression of outdated Gaussians and the refinement of persistent ones, while newly visible surfaces are initialised from learned multi-view depth. We also introduce Multi-State, a benchmark of non-rigid deformation and partial material removal, including synthetic scenes with ground-truth geometry. ReScene achieves quality higher than sequential 3DGS checkpointing with 2.5--4.8$\times$ less uncompressed history storage. At comparable PSNR, it also reduces foreground depth error by 3.2$\times$ relative to sequential ChronoGS, showing that accurate rendering does not necessarily imply accurate geometry. Code and benchmark are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[DominoBench: Evaluating Multi-Step Physical Reasoning from Still Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20385)**  `arXiv:2611.20385`  `cs.CV`  
  _Emilio Ferrara_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Can a vision-language model look at a picture of a chain reaction and tell what will happen? Recent multimodal models describe scenes fluently and solve textbook physics problems, which suggests they should also predict how a scene of ramps, ledges, pendulums, seesaws and dominoes plays out once a ball is pushed. We tested this claim on 30 models from 10 families and reached a clear conclusion: unaided, no model predicts the outcome reliably. We built DominoBench, a benchmark of still images of 2D rigid-body scenes whose outcome a physics simulator computes. It holds 854 items in 427 minimal pairs, in which a change of one to three grid steps in one quantity decides whether one person survives, and every item comes with a four-rung hint ladder ending in the exact calculation that decides the outcome. Unaided, frontier models from Anthropic, Google and OpenAI are right about the person whose fate flips in at most 72% of items and answer both twins of a pair correctly in at most 34%. Pointing at the deciding step helps little, and exact geometry given as text barely helps, yet once the deciding calculation and the quantities it needs, including speeds that a still image cannot show, are written out, the same models reach 82-92% on the deciding person, while small open-weight models stay near chance. Physical prediction from a still image therefore fails at the quantitative step: the strongest models use the deciding quantity when they are given it, but do not produce it on their own. For models that plan or act in the physical world, a fluent account of a scene is thus no evidence of a correct prediction: such models must find and compute the deciding quantity unprompted, and DominoBench tests whether they do.
  </details>

- **[VisDrone-ReID: A Unified Multi-Category Object Re-Identification Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20372)**  `arXiv:2611.20372`  `cs.CV`  
  _Asmau Lawal Bilbis, Nathanael L. Baisa, Amudhavel Jayavel_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Object re-identification (Re-ID) benchmarks remain predominantly confined to single-category evaluations, limiting the development of unified systems capable of operating across diverse object types. This paper presents VisDrone-ReID, a unified multi-category Re-ID benchmark derived from the VisDrone2019-MOT dataset through a principled conversion pipeline. The pipeline resolves structural incompatibilities between multi-object tracking annotations and Re-ID evaluation requirements, addressing sequence-scoped identity collision, temporal redundancy, and the absence of a principled identity-level train/test split. The resulting benchmark comprises 9,516 identities and 150,016 images across ten object categories - pedestrian, people, bicycle, car, van, truck, tricycle, awning-tricycle, bus, and motor, sourced from 80 unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) sequences captured across urban environments. A leave-one-out (LOO) evaluation protocol is adopted to provide a comprehensive assessment. Baseline experiments across four representative Re-ID models spanning CNN and transformer architectures establish the first systematic performance reference on this benchmark. Results reveal a consistent Rank-1 to mAP gap across all models, suggesting that VisDrone-ReID presents a genuine retrieval challenge beyond nearest-neighbour matching. Per-category analysis identifies pedestrian as the most challenging category and reveals that vision-language pretraining provides a measurable advantage on semantically distinctive categories. VisDrone-ReID provides a consistent platform for studying multi-category object re-identification under the scale variation, viewpoint challenges, and category diversity characteristic of real-world surveillance scenarios.
  </details>

- **[Visual Marking as a Native Reasoning Capability for Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20278)**  `arXiv:2611.20278`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiazhen Liu, Yanghao Wang, Chaolei Tan, Long Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual reasoning often requires maintaining intermediate spatial states that are difficult to express in text. Existing approaches either rely on external drawing tools or image generators, or encode these states in latent tokens without explicit spatial correspondence to the input image. We introduce SelfMark, which enables MLLMs to construct and use visual marks directly in their native visual feature space. By treating the visual token grid as a canvas, SelfMark establishes explicit spatial correspondence between marks and images. This is achieved through a locate-mark-read procedure, where the model identifies relevant locations, constructs task-dependent mark features, and reads the resulting visual features to guide its reasoning. We jointly train a single model on spatial planning, dense counting, high-resolution VQA, and line tracing. Across six benchmarks, SelfMark outperforms state-of-the-art MLLM-based methods specialized for individual tasks. These improvements also persist with a stronger backbone, demonstrating the effectiveness of visual marking across different model capabilities. Overall, SelfMark provides a unified approach to visual reasoning by integrating spatially grounded marks directly into the visual feature space of MLLMs.
  </details>

- **[AstroPIE-53: Towards Understanding Failure Modes of Human Pose Estimation in Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20207)**  `arXiv:2611.20207`  `cs.CV`  
  _Stephanie Käs, Marten Flaig, Lukas Woyke, Osmanalp Ömeroğlu, Felix Krueckel, Onur Alp Çulha, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reliable monitoring of astronauts is essential for safety, situational awareness, and autonomous assistance in space missions. While modern computer vision enables accurate human detection and pose estimation on Earth, its robustness in space environments remains largely unquantified due to limited data availability. We present AstroPIE-53, a pilot dataset of 53 annotated astronaut videos collected from publicly available mission footage. To respect copyright restrictions, we release video identifiers, reconstruction scripts, and dense annotations rather than raw videos. The dataset includes bounding boxes, challenge classes, and human-annotated pose failure tags. We benchmark state-of-the-art person detection and pose estimation models under realistic zero-shot deployment conditions and perform the first systematic failure analysis for astronaut monitoring. Our results reveal substantial performance degradation caused by occlusions, unusual body orientations, low contrast, and microgravity motion patterns.
  </details>

- **[GeoReVis: Learning Selective Revision and Evidence Consolidation for Remote Sensing Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20183)**  `arXiv:2611.20183`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ke An, Liang Chen, Yupei Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent methods try to inspect and revise masks to mitigate referential ambiguity and localization errors caused by complex spatial relations in language-guided remote sensing segmentation. However, these solutions often lead to Revision Degradation: revising missing regions may introduce errors into previously correct ones. Even a globally better mask can omit correct regions found in inferior masks. We term this Evidence Fragmentation: correct target evidence is scattered across imperfect masks. All masks generated by the model at this time are suboptimal. We propose GeoReVis, which unifies revision guided by execution feedback, Candidate Selection, and Cross-Mask Evidence Consolidation. It learns fine-grained revision from real actions that improve segmentation when executed and learns to select a better mask through quality comparisons between actual candidates, reducing the risk of degradation during revision. The selected whole mask can still discard useful regions. GeoReVis therefore retains all candidates across rounds, partitions their spatial disagreements into composable regions, and jointly reconstructs regions conditioned on the full instruction and context. This makes complementary and fragmentary evidence reusable regardless of its source candidate's overall quality, yielding improved segmentation. We further build a data pipeline to generate fine-grained multi-round segmentation trajectories and derive supervision for revision actions, candidate selection, and region composition from the model's own outputs, extending training beyond whole-mask IoU supervision. We also propose ReVis-Eval, a benchmark protocol evaluating multi-round revision and cross-round evidence consolidation accuracy. Experiments on ReVis-Eval and 5 benchmarks for remote sensing reasoning and referring segmentation demonstrate the effectiveness of GeoReVis.
  </details>

- **[SGIP: Learning Scene-Grounded Interaction Priors for Scene-Aware Human Motion Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20076)**  `arXiv:2611.20076`  `cs.CV`  
  _Donghyeon Soon, Jiil Yoon, Hyeondong Kim, Daehee Park_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Human motion prediction (HMP) aims to forecast future movement from an observed motion sequence, and recent methods have incorporated 3D scene geometry and object semantics to improve long-term plausibility. However, the destination of future motion remains ambiguous in cluttered scenes: the same observed motion can lead to different interaction targets or navigation modes. Existing methods either handle this ambiguity implicitly or rely on auxiliary signals like gaze that are hard to obtain in practice. To address this, we propose SGIP, which explicitly predicts where in the scene future motion will be grounded across both objects and open navigation regions, and uses it to condition future trajectory and full-body pose prediction. This scene-grounded interaction prior is learned via intent distillation: at training time only, a teacher model scores candidate scene regions by combining an LLM prompted with a textual description of the person's intent with geometric motion-scene features. A student model learns to reproduce these scores from only the observed motion and 3D scene, making intent and LLM unnecessary at test time. Across multiple benchmark datasets, SGIP outperforms prior scene-aware baselines on both trajectory and pose accuracy in a realistic deployment setting where neither gaze nor intent is available at inference, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed framework. The project page is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Glance, then Select: Evidence-Tempered Visual Token Selection for Extremely Compressed Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20006)**  `arXiv:2611.20006`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuxuan Wang, Guangxiang Zhao, Yuxuan Tian, Yahui Han, Xinyu Tan, Feiyu Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) encode a high-resolution image into thousands of visual tokens, which dominate the cost of the prefill stage. Visual token reduction removes most of these tokens, but two problems remain at extreme budgets. The vision encoder still processes every patch of the image, and the few surviving tokens often miss the small regions that hold the answer. In this paper, we propose Glance-ETD, a two-stage token reduction framework guided by a lightweight question-conditioned scorer. In the first stage, a glance encoder views the image at low resolution and re-encodes only the question-relevant regions at native resolution. In the second stage, Evidence-Tempered Diversity (ETD) selects the final tokens by combining diversity with the evidence predicted by the scorer, so that the kept tokens cover the scene and retain the answer. A single-rollout self-distillation recipe trains the MLLM with the glance encoder. With only 5% of the visual tokens, Glance-ETD retains 91.6% of the full-token performance of Qwen3.5-4B across fourteen benchmarks and outperforms the strongest prior method, LT-OPD, by 17.3 points and the same training recipe with DivPrune by 6.0. It also speeds up the time to first token on 4K images by 3.1x and generalizes to three other MLLMs and to high-resolution document benchmarks, with the largest gains on text-rich tasks.
  </details>

- **[Two-Stage Prompt Learning: A Unified Framework for Person ReID without Clothing Hypothesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19939)**  `arXiv:2611.19939`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuxuan Xiao, Shanshan Zhang, Shengcai Liao, Jian Yang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Person re-identification (ReID) in real-world scenarios involves three clothes conditions: the same clothes (SC), changed clothes (CC), and the unknown (GEN) scenario where clothes changes are not specified. Existing methods typically train separate SC- or CC-specific models, which limits generalization across all conditions. Meanwhile, pre-trained visual-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP have shown strong generalization ability in various vision tasks, motivating recent ReID methods to incorporate textual prompts. However, to distinguish SC and CC, prior approaches rely on fixed predefined instructions, which lack learnable context embeddings in prompts and cannot effectively handle the GEN scenario. To overcome these limitations, we propose Two-Stage Prompt Learning (TSPL), a unified framework that integrates clothes-identity decoupling and scenario-aware task prompts within VLMs. In Stage 1, appearance-decomposed prompt learning disentangles clothes and identity information under frozen encoders, producing stable embeddings where clothes cues can be selectively preserved or suppressed. In Stage 2, task-aware prompt learning trains the image encoder with learnable context embeddings guided by Stage-1 representations, enabling robust adaptation across SC, CC, and GEN conditions. Extensive experiments on five benchmarks show that TSPL achieves state-of-the-art performance under all scenarios, improving mAP by up to 9.2% on LTCC (SC) and by 6.4% on VC-Clothes (GEN), respectively. The code will be released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[HEmIF-Bench: Benchmarking H&E-to-mIF Models BeyondVirtual-StainFidelity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19905)**  `arXiv:2611.19905`  `cs.CV`  
  _Fuxian Zhao, Hyokeun Park_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Hematoxylin and eosin (H&E)-to-multiplex immunofluorescence (mIF) generators predict spatial protein expression from routine histology, yet pixel-level marker reconstruction does not establish downstream utility. We present HEmIF-Bench, evaluating five frozen generators across eight datasets spanning molecular, cellular, tissue, and patient scales. HEmIF-Bench decouples internal encoder features from generated outputs, benchmarking both against the pathology foundation reference UNI2. Mirroring the clinical workflow where pathologists rely on mIF to define cell phenotypes, delineate tertiary lymphoid structures (TLSs) and germinal centers (GCs), and stratify patient risk, we evaluate external CD3 recovery, single-cell classification, dense nuclear segmentation, TLS/GC segmentation, and patient survival prediction. Across the benchmark, foundation-model-based MIPHEI-ViT leads among the generators, while non-foundation models show task-specific strengths. Comparing the two routes reveals that internal encoder features generally outperform generated outputs, and larger output marker panels do not guarantee stronger downstream utility across datasets. Moreover, neither internal encoder features nor generated outputs consistently surpass frozen UNI2. These findings show that pixel-level stain fidelity does not equate to downstream utility, and that generators trained at current paired-data scales have yet to surpass large-scale pure-H&E foundation models. With the expansion of paired translation cohorts and the advancement of foundation-based generative architectures, HEmIF-Bench establishes a definitive benchmark for tracking when H&E-to-mIF generation crosses the threshold to deliver genuine value beyond routine histology for tumor microenvironment analysis and precision oncology.
  </details>

- **[PRISM: Primitive-Integrated Separable Modulation with Priority Visual Memory Queue for Compositional Zero-Shot Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19828)**  `arXiv:2611.19828`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jianhua Wang, Zhihang Zheng, Yang Liu, Jiale Du_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Compositional Zero-Shot Learning (CZSL) recognizes unseen attribute--object pairs by recombining primitives from seen compositions. Existing methods stop sharing evidence at deployment: fixed prototypes cannot exploit unlabeled test streams, while composition-wise refinement cannot propagate corrections across pairs sharing a primitive. We introduce PRISM, an online CZSL framework that extends primitive sharing to test-time adaptation. Its Hierarchical Knowledge Accumulation Module (HiKAM) factorizes prototype migration into composition-, attribute-, and object-level residual banks. A Priority Visual Memory Queue (PVMQ), initialized by Prototype Queue Initialization (PQI), grounds text updates with bounded visual evidence. The same adaptive core supports exhaustive Full PRISM and recall-calibrated Adaptive PRISM-Lite, which selects candidate budgets from held-out ranks. Comprehensive closed- and open-world experiments on UT-Zappos, MIT-States, and C-GQA demonstrate the state of the art, with Full PRISM achieving the best open-world AUC and harmonic mean on all three benchmarks and the best closed-world results on UT-Zappos and C-GQA. Fine-grained long-tail and non-stationary primitive-block evaluations reveal gains on under-supported concepts and stable adaptation under prolonged skew. Adaptive PRISM-Lite further delivers substantial efficiency gains in the full open-world space with only a small accuracy cost. PRISM thus combines primitive-shared online adaptation, multimodal grounding, and candidate-aware inference.
  </details>

- **[When Does Reusing Layers Help Multimodal Embeddings?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19827)**  `arXiv:2611.19827`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhizhang Hu, Yue Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We ask whether a compact universal multimodal embedding models can produce better search embeddings by running the same layers again. Looped transformers add computation with nearly unchanged model size, making them an appealing alternative to a larger encoder. Their success in language-model reasoning and recent use for retrieval motivate the idea, but higher retrieval scores alone do not reveal what an extra pass contributes. We study eleven loop designs and training variants on three checkpoints of one vision-language architecture. Our protocol distinguishes whether a model uses its extra pass, whether its image states respond to the query, and whether those updates improve retrieval. We prove that an image placed before the query remains query-independent through repeated layers when information only travels forward. We then open a query-to-image path in three ways, including order-alternating recurrence, which places the query first on the next pass. Each path produces query-sensitive image states. For bidirectional attention and order alternation, freezing later visual updates does not lower accuracy. On all 78 datasets of the Massive Multimodal Embedding Benchmark version 2 (MMEB-V2), a standard added loop loses 0.46 points against its trained control; an intermediate generative stage changes the effect to +0.36. A model pretrained with recurrence gains +4.23 points over its own loop-removed control and benefits from query-conditioned image updates when answering visual questions or locating objects from text.
  </details>

- **[Pros4D: Modeling Evolving 4D Worlds in Latent Space for Dynamic Spatial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19803)**  `arXiv:2611.19803`  `cs.CV`  
  _Rongxin Gao, Dongxuan Liu, Chu Li, Chengyang Ji, Jialing Huang, Runyu Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Dynamic spatial reasoning from monocular video enables vision-language models (VLMs) to understand how 3D environments evolve over time, supporting physical world understanding and embodied intelligence. Existing efforts primarily address observed-dynamics understanding, whereas agents in dynamic environments also require future-dynamics prediction beyond the observation boundary. Moreover, external geometric processing and textual descriptions can increase inference complexity or lose information when representing continuous dynamics. These limitations motivate a shared internal state that preserves observed dynamics and remains predictive beyond the input video. Accordingly, we present Pros4D, a framework for modeling evolving 4D worlds in latent space that unifies retrospective understanding of observed-dynamics with prospective reasoning for future-dynamics prediction. Specifically, Pros4D-Engine derives geometry-grounded supervision and constructs paired examples for both tasks, while Pros4D-Bench evaluates them across eight spatial task categories spanning dynamic cameras, objects, and scenes. At the model level, Pros4D compresses observed dynamics into latent observed states and autoregressively rolls them forward into latent future states. 4D Latent Alignment (4DLA) grounds these states through complementary visual-semantic and geometric supervision, while joint language supervision connects them to task reasoning. Experiments across benchmarks show consistent improvements over strong baselines in both capabilities.
  </details>

- **[Fine-Grained RLAIF for Visual Safety: Learning to Zoom into Hazards for Multimodal Safety Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19786)**  `arXiv:2611.19786`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chunxue Xu, Yiwei Wang, Yujun Cai, Songze Li_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent multimodal safety alignment heavily relies on a text-centric paradigm, assuming risks are explicit in language or globally prominent in images. However, real-world hazards are often visually subtle or peripheral, causing existing models to fail due to their reliance on coarse, global visual representations. In this work, we study fine-grained visual safety alignment and introduce a challenging 1,200-sample benchmark across four domains, where hazards necessitate localized inspection. To address this, we propose Fine-Grained RLAIF (FGR), a reinforcement learning framework that casts image zoom-in as a learnable perception policy. FGR employs a decomposed reward design to jointly align three core safety behaviors: (i) where to look via a visual evidence reward, (ii) when to act via a tool-formatting constraint, and (iii) how to reason via visually grounded safety explanations. FGR achieves accuracy ranging from 86.71% to 93.00%, consistently outperforming strong multimodal and agent-based baselines.
  </details>

- **[A Threshold-Free Visual-Semantic Matching Metric for Evaluating Consistency in Subject-Driven Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19780)**  `arXiv:2611.19780`  `cs.CV`  
  _Bowei Shan, Heng Yin_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In subject-driven text-to-image generation, quantitatively evaluating localized visual consistency across varying poses and environments remains a major challenge. Existing metrics either rely on global image embeddings (CLIP, DINO) and Vision-Language Models (VLMs) that pool spatial representations and miss fine-grained textural artifacts, or depend on scalar threshold-gated dense metrics such as Visual-Semantic Matching (VSM) that suffer from point-alignment dropouts, severe threshold sensitivity, and score instability. To overcome these limitations, we propose TF-VSM, a fully continuous, threshold-free visual-consistency metric paired with an extended feature-disentanglement retraining schedule for dual-branch ResNet feature-aggregation networks. Rather than enforcing rigid binary cutoffs $(T_s,T_v)$, TF-VSM weights every object point by a non-linear semantic match confidence $w_j=ReLU(\hat{s}_j)^2$ and continuously integrates visual similarity over all consistency levels $r\in[0,1]$, yielding a closed-form confidence-weighted score without any hand-tuned hyperparameters. On a controlled 100-pair benchmark with ground-truth inpainting oracles, TF-VSM attains Pearson $0.638$ and Spearman $0.674$ oracle correlation, improving over the pretrained VSM baseline ($0.454/0.589$) by $+0.171/+0.116$ after retraining, and drastically outperforming CLIP ($-0.052/-0.004$), DINO ($0.087/0.121$), and VLMs ($0.096/0.003$). Six diagnostic ablations confirm that smooth confidence weighting is the right mechanism, that hard semantic gating is brittle (dropping to $0.533$ Pearson at $T_s{=}0.8$), and that the threshold-free metric is essentially invariant to the training-time loss balance $\alpha$ that the thresholded metric still depends on.
  </details>

- **[SEAT: Sign-Type Evidence-Audited Transport for Multi-Label Tongue Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19748)**  `arXiv:2611.19748`  `cs.CV`  
  _Liuyi Xu, Qing Wang, Zikai Liang, An-Yang Lu, Yining Qian_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Automated tongue-sign recognition can improve the consistency of non-invasive examinations that otherwise rely on subjective visual judgment. A single tongue image often exhibits multiple coexisting signs characterized by heterogeneous evidence, including localized morphology, global chromaticity, and inter-label dependence. However, existing methods typically rely on shared representations and uniform multi-label heads, making it difficult to verify whether predictions stem from clinically relevant evidence rather than spurious visual correlations. We propose SEAT, an evidence-audited framework that matches distinct evidence types with dedicated prediction and evaluation mechanisms. Built on frozen DINOv2 patch features, SEAT couples mask-guided sign-specific attention for localized morphology, a semi-relaxed optimal transport pathway over LAB/HSV color prototypes to enhance pale-tongue recognition, and a co-occurrence-aware transport regularizer to shape the batch-level predicted label marginal. These components are evaluated through audits matched to their evidence types. On the TongueDx benchmark, SEAT achieves a mean macro-F1 of 72.59 with a standard deviation of 1.04 across six random seeds, with a six-model ensemble reaching 74.15. The ensemble outperforms a backbone-matched baseline and a larger, domain-specialized vision-language model by 8.06 and 2.99 percentage points, respectively. Our results demonstrate that aligning dedicated prediction pathways and audits with sign-specific characteristics improves multi-label accuracy while offering more targeted evidential grounding than uniform post-hoc spatial explanations. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[EndoComparer: Comparative Surgical Video Question Answering with Pseudo-Siamese Visual Adaptation and Perceive-then-Compare Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19578)**  `arXiv:2611.19578`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shi Li, Lalith Sharan, Joël L. Lavanchy, Kun Yuan, Lorenzo Arboit, Nabani Banik, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Purpose: Surgical training benefits from explicit comparison between cases: in patient condition, in technique and in skill proficiency. Surgical vision-language models (VLMs) offer a promising and flexible interface for this purpose. Yet current VLMs typically see one video at a time, and they often fall short when asked to compare videos. Methods: We propose EndoComparer, a VLM for comparative surgical video question answering with two components. (a) A Pseudo-Siamese Visual LoRA (PSVL) gives each video slot its own low-rank adapter over a shared frozen vision encoder, separating the two videos in the visual pathway at the cost of a single encoder pass. (b) A Perceive-then-Compare chain of thought (PtC-CoT) trains the model to first state what each video shows, then derive the answer from these statements. We further curate EndoDiffVQA, a benchmark of nearly 11K multiple-choice questions on video-pair over cholecystectomy and gastric bypass procedures, covering anatomy, tool, action and skill. Results: EndoComparer outperforms strong generalist and surgical VLMs on detecting and specifying difference between videos. Ablations show that two proposed components contribute complimentarily, and the model remains more robust under a video-swap control that exposes confusion between the two videos. Conclusion: Providing a dedicated visual reasoning path through PSVL and PtC-CoT makes cross-case comparison of surgical videos more robust for VLMs. EndoDiffVQA and EndoComparer provide a benchmark and a strong baseline for this task. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[PALIN: Pixel-Aligned LiDAR-NIR Multimodal 3D Object Detection with Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19485)**  `arXiv:2611.19485`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yanan Zhang, Amit Vishnia, Chengbo Ai, Eleni Christofa, Runan Cheng, Siyuan Meng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D object detection is critical for intelligent transportation systems. LiDAR has been widely adopted for accurate geometric measurements; however, purely geometric representations remain ambiguous under sparse observations and occlusions, while cross-sensor multimodal fusion suffers from calibration and synchronization errors. To overcome these challenges, we leverage an Ouster LiDAR to construct PALIN (Pixel-Aligned LiDAR-NIR), a large-scale intrinsically pixel-aligned multimodal dataset jointly capturing geometry and NIR intensity, comprising approximately 8,000 annotated frames from static urban intersections and diverse mobile suburban scenes. Building upon this dataset, we propose CMambaFusion, a novel fusion framework consisting of a four-directional Cross-Mamba module for bidirectional cross-modal interaction and a Gated Concat-Mamba module for joint feature refinement. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PALIN enables effective multimodal learning, and CMambaFusion achieves superior accuracy-efficiency trade-offs over both LiDAR-only and representative fusion baselines.
  </details>

- **[StyleJudge: A Human-Validated, In-Context-Learning Vision-Language Evaluator for Artistic Style Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19475)**  `arXiv:2611.19475`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lokesh, Pramukto Mandal, Somanshu Juneja, Jashnoor Singh, Amit Kumar, Pranav Adlinge, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Automatic evaluation of artistic style transfer remains an open problem: existing metrics score an image pair in isolation, without seeing an example of the intended style transformation, and correlate poorly with human judgment. We introduce StyleJudge, a compact vision-language evaluator conditioned on a small set of reference source-to-stylized pairs before scoring a query pair, mirroring how a human rater calibrates to a style before judging a candidate. On a human-annotated benchmark of 878 rated pairs across 12 artistic styles (728 from 10 training/validation styles, 150 from 2 unseen styles), our efficient 0.8B-parameter StyleJudge reaches a Pearson correlation of 0.291 (Spearman 0.325) with human ratings on seen styles and 0.381 on unseen ones. On seen styles, it achieves the highest positive correlation among tested metrics: Gram-matrix style loss and CLIP similarity correlate weakly ($r=0.041$, $r=0.168$), while LPIPS (scored higher when closer to the source) correlates negatively ($r=-0.404$). We achieve this by distilling evaluation knowledge from a 72B-parameter vision-language teacher used only during training, which reaches $r=0.327$, confirming that most of its human alignment survives compact distillation. For training, we build a dataset of source-to-stylized pairs across the 12 styles via LoRA-adapted diffusion, densified with graded intermediate examples from a high-resolution image-morphing pipeline so the evaluator recognizes partial and complete stylization. On two held-out validation styles, StyleJudge reaches 28.2% exact-match agreement with its teacher's structured scores versus 1.1% for the untuned base model, with 67.8% shorter reasoning traces, and scores a pair in 39 s (median) using 2.5 GB of GPU memory on one NVIDIA T4. We release the human-rating benchmark, annotation guideline, SDXL-adapted FreeMorph code, and model weights.
  </details>

- **[RETR: A Structure-Preserving RGB-Event Transformer for Robust 3D Lane Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19358)**  `arXiv:2611.19358`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jingtao Dong, Hao Zhuang, Hao Yang, Liyuan Pan, Wei Liang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Robust 3D lane detection requires accurate metric road geometry recovery from visual inputs, yet conventional RGB-based approaches suffer from fragile lane evidence under adverse illumination, motion blur, and long-range perspective compression. Event cameras offer complementary high-temporal-resolution, high dynamic-range structural cues that are robust to these challenges, but their sparse, motion-dependent responses cannot be directly fused into consistent 3D geometry. In this paper, we present the first attempt to introduce event cameras into 3D lane detection. To enable systematic research, we first build two multimodal benchmarks with metric 3D lane annotations: DSEC-3DLD (real-world sequences) and Ev-OpenLane (large-scale simulated sequences). We further propose RETR, a structure preserving transformer that converts complementary RGB and event observations into coherent 3D lane geometry. RETR first aligns modality consistent evidence through Reciprocal Context Flow Fusion, then preserves thin and uncertain lane structures with Uncertainty Aware Structural Consolidation, and finally decodes ordered lane hypotheses using a Geometry State Decoder with proposal conditioned initialization, reference conditioned query evolution, and diversified geometric inquiry. Extensive experiments show that RETR achieves state-of-the-art performance on both benchmarks, especially showing strong improvements in challenging lighting and complex road geometry scenarios. Code and dataset will be available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Generate, Align, and Restore: MLLM-Empowered Diffusion for Generalizable Low-light Image Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19285)**  `arXiv:2611.19285`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kaisheng Pang, Feng Li, Chen Zhang, Junjun Jiang, Haoang Li, Sam Kwong, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Low-light image enhancement (LLIE) is essential to numerous vision applications operating under adverse illumination. Despite substantial advances, real-world nighttime enhancement remains challenging due to the scarcity of strictly aligned day-night pairs and complex degradations involving extreme darkness and heterogeneous artificial light sources, leading to fragile generalization. To overcome these challenges, we propose MrLIE (MLLM-empowered reference Low-light Image Enhancement), a novel reference-based unsupervised LLIE framework that synergizes the generative and reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) with conditional diffusion. MrLIE employs the MLLM as a high-fidelity reference synthesizer and diagnostic critic, iteratively generating, diagnosing, and correcting normal-light references directly from degraded nighttime observations. Based on this, our method governs the enhancement process through two complementary mechanisms, in which a confidence offset module rectifies spatial-geometric inconsistencies for reference reliability and a structure-anchored injection scheme harmonizes aligned reference cues, low-light structural constraints, and ControlNet additive anchoring during the denoising process. Extensive experiments on diverse benchmarks demonstrate that MrLIE achieves superior perceptual quality, natural appearance, and robust generalization.
  </details>

- **[LC-ISP Benchmark: Learning Advanced ISP with Live and Capture Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19284)**  `arXiv:2611.19284`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kai Chen, Junyu Lou, Jinhua Zhang, Weiyi You, Shuhang Gu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  High-quality paired RAW-to-sRGB datasets are essential for data-driven ISP, yet existing datasets often rely on cross-device collection, causing misalignment and failing to faithfully reflect real capture conditions. We therefore introduce LC-ISP, a large-scale benchmark built from the intrinsic gap between a camera's live preview and offline capture streams. LC-ISP contains 53.9k spatially aligned groups, each comprising live RAW/live sRGB and multi-frame fused RGB/capture sRGB, and covers day, night, indoor, outdoor, and HDR scenes. It also provides capture-stream intermediates for modular supervision. We further develop a compact two-stage baseline that uses these intermediates as auxiliary targets. Under a 2.5-GFLOP budget for 448$\times$448 RAW inputs, it outperforms the strongest single-frame baseline by over 2 dB PSNR while improving texture, denoising, and color reproduction. LC-ISP supports realistic benchmarking, the design of supervision strategies, and the development of lightweight ISP models.
  </details>

- **[Do VLMs Really Look at the Road? A Diagnostic Evaluation for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19184)**  `arXiv:2611.19184`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shankar Gangisetty, Muskan Singh, C V Jawahar_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly being evaluated for autonomous driving through question-answering benchmarks, yet high benchmark accuracy does not necessarily reflect visually grounded reasoning. We present a diagnostic evaluation framework that systematically probes whether VLMs base safety-critical driving decisions on visual evidence or instead rely on language priors, answer biases, and dataset regularities. Our benchmark comprises two task categories, Adverse Weather and Junctions \& Intersections, with a nuScenes spatial reasoning extension, and evaluates open-source (general, driving, and spatial-reasoning) VLMs along four diagnostic protocols: image versus no-image evaluation, counterfactual weather sensitivity, linguistic robustness under paraphrasing and negation, and spatial reasoning. All ground-truth labels are derived programmatically from BDD100K and DriveLM annotations, enabling reproducible evaluation without manual labeling. Across all models, we find that competitive overall accuracy conceals systematic failure modes, including strong affirmation bias, limited sensitivity to counterfactual scene changes, near-chance performance on negated questions despite high paraphrase consistency, and confident driving decisions even without visual input. These results demonstrate that current open-source VLMs remain insufficiently grounded for safety-critical autonomous driving and underscore the need for diagnostic evaluation beyond aggregate accuracy.
  </details>

- **[DEFDVO: Deformable Deep Visual Odometry for Equirectangular Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19095)**  `arXiv:2611.19095`  `cs.CV`  
  _Burak Sevsay, Egor Bondarev, Erkut Akdag_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Omnidirectional ($360^\circ$) cameras capture the full surrounding scene in every frame, providing a strong advantage for visual odometry (VO) over pinhole sensors with a limited field of view. However, their equirectangular projection (ERP) introduces latitude-dependent distortion, an artificial seam at the image border, and a sampling bias toward the poles. Existing $360^\circ$ VO methods address these challenges only partially. DEFDVO closes this gap with a patch-based deep VO method that operates directly on ERP imagery, combining spherically initialized deformable feature extraction, end-to-end seam-aware estimation, and spherical-gradient patch selection. The deformable feature extractor combines spherical geometric priors with learned sampling offsets, while seam-aware processing preserves ERP continuity, and spherical-gradient selection reduces polar sampling bias. Trained exclusively on synthetic TartanAirV2 ERP imagery, DEFDVO improves the best prior average absolute trajectory error (ATE) by $4.8\times$ (0.201 m to 0.042 m) on the TartanAirV2 evaluation set defined by 360DVO and achieves the lowest average ATE on both splits of the real-world 360DVO benchmark. Code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[SearchChord: Prompt Preselection and Field Recomposition for One-Step Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19076)**  `arXiv:2611.19076`  `cs.CV`  
  _Boyang Deng, Yuzhen Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Finding effective prompts and seeds for one-step image editors requires balancing edit fidelity, source preservation, and computational cost. To address this challenge, we explore a large collection of candidate edits, where each candidate is defined by a unique prompt-seed pair, and generate corresponding images on demand. We present SearchChord, a training-free framework that leverages a large pool of such candidates, combining text-based preselection, image verification, and latent-field recomposition. Our ablation studies across pool sizes up to 200 candidates show that a pool of 100 offers a cost-effective operating point. Compared with feeding all 100 candidates into recomposition, TextSim preselects a subset of 10 candidates. Rather than uniform averaging, we incorporate image-verifier scores to weight each candidate during recomposition. Experiments on PIE-Bench and the proposed CropWeed-EditBench reveal a trade-off: candidate selection improves target-text alignment, while recomposition better preserves unmodified source content. The oracle verifier achieves the best performance in both preservation and target-text alignment. Practical verification partially recovers semantic alignment, but recomposition still compromises edit fidelity, including in agricultural image edits. These results motivate text-first preselection and stronger verification to balance preservation, fidelity, and cost. Code and data will be made publicly available.
  </details>

- **[CISyn: Human Mesh Recovery in Close Interactions with Physics-Simulated Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18894)**  `arXiv:2611.18894`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yufan Deng, Lorenza Prospero, Christian Rupprecht_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D human mesh recovery (HMR) in close-interaction scenarios is hindered by the lack of large-scale, accurately annotated training data: real capture is expensive, and existing synthetic datasets rarely include physical contact. We introduce CISyn, a large-scale synthetic dataset purpose-built for close human-human interactions, generated via physics simulation and Blender-based rendering. By simulating collision dynamics between SMPL-X-rigged humanoids, CISyn naturally produces the contact, mutual occlusion, and pose entanglement that characterize close-interaction scenes, with no manual annotation effort. The released set provides 25,000 sequences with accurate 3D annotations. We further propose an uncertainty-aware loss that allows models to learn more effectively from the heavily occluded examples in our data. Augmenting state-of-the-art HMR training with CISyn and replacing the regression loss with our uncertainty-aware variant yields consistent improvements on close-interaction benchmarks, validating CISyn as a scalable complement to real-world data. The dataset, generation pipeline, and code are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[RAVEN-Bench: A Paired EO-IR Video QA Benchmark for Aerial Multimodal Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18880)**  `arXiv:2611.18880`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yu Hu, Jianyang Gu, Yue Cao, Hao Liu, Kangnan Wang, Jozsef Hamari, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have advanced rapidly in image and video understanding, yet their ability to reason over paired aerial electro-optical and infrared (EO-IR) videos remains poorly understood. Such video poses a harder test than standard RGB video QA: targets are often small, evidence may appear only briefly, viewpoints change with the platform, and useful cues may appear in only one stream. We introduce RAVEN-Bench (Reasoning over Aerial Videos with EO-IR aligNment), a paired EO-IR video QA benchmark for low-altitude aerial multimodal understanding. RAVEN-Bench contains 100 temporally aligned EO-IR video pairs and 1,200 human-authored four-way multiple-choice questions, each independently audited for answer keys, modality requirements, evidence intervals, and group validity. It supports EO-only, IR-only, and paired EO+IR evaluation, and uses hierarchical capability levels and structured question groups to diagnose paired-input gain beyond the stronger unimodal condition, unimodal reliance, target consistency, and evidence-chain coherence. We evaluate frontier, open-weight, and remote-sensing MLLMs, showing that aggregate accuracy can overstate evidence-supported EO-IR reasoning. RAVEN-Bench provides controlled modality conditions and group-level diagnostics for auditing whether progress on RGB video benchmarks transfers to aerial cross-spectral understanding. Code and data are available atthis https URLandthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[UnSPAD: Uncertainty-aware Single Photon Burst Photography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18869)**  `arXiv:2611.18869`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sachin Shah, Kalyan Bhetwal, Levi Burner, Christopher A. Metzler_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We introduce UnSPAD, an uncertainty-aware burst photography framework capable of fusing and denoising arbitrarily long sequences of binary single photon avalanche diode (SPAD) measurements. Unlike prior methods that require fixed burst lengths or rely on powerful generative priors, UnSPAD combines learned motion estimation with uncertainty-informed measurement fusion and denoising to produce temporally consistent, hallucination-free reconstructions. UnSPAD first forms an initial scene estimate by averaging flow-aligned binary frames. UnSPAD then uses per-pixel uncertainty maps to perform uncertainty-aware denoising; the denoiser is informed about which regions accumulated the fewest observations (and thus contain the most relative shot noise) due to motion, occlusion, or alignment errors. This two-step reconstruction pipeline helps mitigate hallucinations---outside of the structure implicitly introduced by the flow estimates, the initial scenes estimates are entirely prior-free. Experiments on real and simulated SPAD and quantum image sensor (QIS) benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art reconstruction quality, improved temporal consistency, and superior color fidelity relative to the state of the art.
  </details>

- **[FiReIQ: Field-Level Confidence Estimation with Reasoning and Image Quality in Document Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18865)**  `arXiv:2611.18865`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiao Liu, Yage Wang, Qi Yu, Giri Iyengar_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have demonstrated strong performance in structured document extraction, yet their deployment in high-stakes applications requires reliable confidence estimates for individual fields. Existing uncertainty estimators often aggregate signals across an entire generation, obscuring field-specific reasoning uncertainty while overlooking visual degradations that may lead to confident extraction errors. To address these limitations, we introduce FiReIQ, a field-level confidence estimation framework that combines field-aligned reasoning signals from a single VLM generation with local and global image-quality features through quality-conditioned visual fusion. To facilitate systematic evaluation, we further introduce FiReIQ-Bench, a benchmark spanning financial receipts and legal contracts. Experiments with two reasoning VLMs show that FiReIQ consistently improves confidence discrimination, selective prediction, and probabilistic accuracy over existing estimators. These gains extend to private financial service data, where FiReIQ improves AUROC by 6.8% and coverage at 95% precision by 10.6% over an existing baseline. Together, these results highlight the potential of FiReIQ to reduce manual review while enabling more reliable document automation.
  </details>

- **[Scaling Channel-Adaptive Self-Supervised Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18849)**  `arXiv:2611.18849`  `cs.CV`  
  _Alice V. De Lorenci, Seungeun Yi, Theo Moutakanni, Piotr Bojanowski, Camille Couprie, Juan C. Caicedo, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent advances in self-supervised pre-training of foundation models for natural images have made them a popular choice for various visual systems and applications. Self-supervised strategies are also promising in non-RGB scientific imaging domains such as in biology, medical and satellite imagery, but their broader application is hampered by heterogeneity in channel composition and semantics between relevant datasets: two datasets may contain different numbers of channels, and these may reveal distinct aspects of an object or scene. Recent works on channel adaptive strategies report substantial advantages for those that account for variable channel compositions without sacrificing the ability to jointly encode channels; yet, how these strategies behave at scale remains unclear. We here show that, surprisingly, trained across large-scale datasets, independent-encoding of channels outperforms joint-encoding methods by a substantial margin. We validate this result along an extensive set of experiments on various datasets from cell microscopy to geospatial imagery. Our DINO BoC approach sets a new state-of-the-art across challenging benchmarks, including generalization to out-of-distribution tasks and unseen channel combinations. We open-source code and model weights for a new general-purpose feature extractor for fluorescent microscopy.
  </details>

- **[From Retouched Images to Paired Videos: A Synthetic Benchmark for Face Video Retouching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18791)**  `arXiv:2611.18791`  `cs.CV`  
  _Peiyuan Zeng, Fengchuang Xing, Shiming Wu, Hongwei Yue, Chao Zhou_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Paired unretouched/retouched face videos are costly to produce because professional editing must remain consistent over time, while releasing identifiable face footage raises privacy, consent, and governance concerns.
